| After 2014 through 2016Q1 | Lower oil prices reduced cash flow; investment at sample companies contracted faster, improving the funding gap, but output was also affected by depletion at existing fields and fewer new wells | Free-cash-flow improvement may come from reduced reinvestment, rather than recovering demand or operations |
| 2018 | The United States became the largest crude-oil producer but remained a net crude-oil importer that year | Success in production technology, the trade structure and "no need to import" must not be conflated |
| 2020-06-28 | Chesapeake filed for Chapter 11 reorganization | It cannot be incorrectly placed in a list of 2014-2016 bankruptcies |
| 2024-05-03 | ExxonMobil completed its acquisition of Pioneer | Consolidation is a fact of another stage; the two can no longer be listed as independent competitors today |

The cash flow sample above comprised 39 US onshore crude-oil producers in the EIA's 2016 article. In 2016Q1 they produced approximately 2.10 million barrels/day, about 30% of output in the contiguous 48 states. It does not represent all US exploration and production companies, still less the global energy industry. [EIA: Onshore oil-company capital investment and operating cash flow, 2016-07-18](https://www.eia.gov/todayinenergy/detail.php?id=27112)

For production ranking and trade conclusions, see respectively [EIA review of 2018 crude-oil production](https://www.eia.gov/todayinenergy/detail.php?id=37820) and [EIA 2018 energy production, imports and exports](https://www.eia.gov/todayinenergy/detail.php?id=39392). Chesapeake's date comes from its [2020-06-28 company SEC announcement](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/895126/000110465920077745/tm2023599d1_ex99-1.htm); Pioneer's consolidation date comes from [ExxonMobil's acquisition-completion announcement](https://corporate.exxonmobil.com/news/news-releases/2024/0503_ExxonMobil-completes-acquisition-of-Pioneer-Natural-Resources).

Bankruptcy statistics are particularly prone to mixing different samples. Cobalt entered Chapter 11 on December 14, 2017; its business was primarily deepwater oil and gas, so it cannot illustrate bankruptcies of pure shale-oil companies in 2014-2016. Industry bankruptcy counts and bond returns both require the company list, period and asset class to be fixed first. [Cobalt 2017 10-K](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1471261/000156459018004275/cie-10k_20171231.htm)

### II. Six Comparison Dimensions: Similar Mechanisms Do Not Mean Identical Outcomes

| Dimension | Shale-oil observation | Corresponding question in AI research | Where direct analogy fails |
|---|---|---|---|
| Technology and supply | Greater production efficiency and new supply affect market balance | Improved compute and model efficiency allow more tasks to be served | Barrels of oil are relatively easy to standardize; AI tasks differ in quality, latency and reliability |
| Timing gap between investment and cash | New wells require investment first, with recovery later depending on output and prices | Equipment, data centers and power are funded before billable use develops | Mature cloud-provider businesses can cross-fund investment; financing structures differ |
| Asset consumption and renewal | Wells suffer physical production decline | Chips face physical wear and economic obsolescence, while data-center and energy assets have longer lives | The same depreciation curve and remaining life cannot be applied to both |
| Cost and price | Crude prices may fall faster than costs | Prices for tasks of equivalent quality, unit inference costs and usage change together | API price cuts do not automatically mean lower profits; costs and elasticity must be examined |
| Financing and residual value | Collateral, bonds, hedging and maturity structures affect resilience | Corporate bonds, leases, guarantees, prepayments and equity financing are intertwined | Investment-grade groups and emerging compute operators cannot share one default model |
| Value distribution | Consumers, low-cost companies, oilfield services and creditors earn different returns | Applications, cloud, chips, energy and end customers compete for gains | A business winner need not be a stock-market winner at any price |

Placing peak US E&P capital expenditure beside the budgets of four cloud providers looks intuitive, but the samples, asset scopes, price levels and years differ; it cannot establish an equivalent degree of bubble. A scale comparison should harmonize the currency-value year and industry boundaries, and separate new investment from replacement investment. Until then, no unverified large number is added to strengthen the analogy.

### III. "Selling Shovels" Is Not a Business Model Immune to the Cycle

#### Oilfield Services Themselves Provide a Counterexample

Charging service fees does not insulate oilfield services from oil prices: when producers drill less, service volumes, equipment utilization and bargaining power all change. SLB reported 2016 revenue of USD 27.810 billion, down approximately 22% from USD 35.475 billion in 2015; its disclosed measure of pretax operating income fell from USD 6.510 billion to USD 3.273 billion, approximately 50%. The 2016 period also included nine months of Cameron after its acquisition. The decline therefore cannot be treated as a purely like-for-like organic decrease, but is sufficient to refute the assertion that it can "earn stable profits regardless of oil prices." [SLB full-year 2016 results announcement](https://www.slb.com/newsroom/press-release/2017/pr-2017-0120-q4-earnings)

Pipeline businesses also require contractual distinctions. Contracts with minimum payments, long-term volume commitments or credit support behave differently from those charging mainly for actual transported volumes when customers fail or production declines. Even where cash fees are relatively stable, leverage, project construction costs and purchase valuation still affect investor returns. Without a complete same-period total-return sample, one cannot assert that the entire oilfield-service or midstream sector necessarily earns more than producers.

#### NVIDIA and Oilfield Services: Compare Order Dependence, Not Occupational Metaphors

NVIDIA's hardware, software ecosystem, interconnects and development tools form a set of competitive factors different from traditional oilfield services. It is not a drilling contractor paid simply by hours worked. Yet upstream suppliers remain exposed if downstream customers cut equipment budgets, change architectures or delay data-center commissioning. Selling essential equipment does not automatically remove order risk caused by customers' failure to recover cash.

Customer concentration must be read according to disclosure definitions: direct customers may be distributors or system manufacturers, while end users may be cloud providers or may purchase through dedicated compute operators. Direct-customer shares are not treated as end-customer shares, and no calculation is made of how much riskier NVIDIA is than an oilfield-service company before entities and periods are aligned.

The testing sequence is whether end-customer construction plans have changed, whether delivery and payment have changed, whether alternative architectures erode unit value, and whether suppliers continue supporting purchases through financing arrangements. The residual-value guarantees and purchase warrants discussed in this chapter show that upstream earnings and downstream credit can be connected beyond equipment sales. This is closer to the substance of risk-bearing than simply comparing who is the "shovel seller."

### IV. Technological Efficiency, Prices and Replacement Speed: Where the Analogy Most Easily Breaks Down

#### Oil Volume and Price Are Not Token Volume and Price

Oil prices are an important common market signal, whereas AI-service pricing depends on the task, model quality, input/output mix, caching, batching, reasoning depth and latency requirements. The same one million tokens may not produce the same work outcome, so the cheapest listed price alone should not be used to judge whether the whole application market has become commoditized.

Improved model efficiency can lower unit costs and expand usage, and can also trigger competitive price cuts. The direction of total revenue and profit depends on their relative pace. Illustratively, if the price of a comparable task falls 15% and paid usage rises 20%, the revenue change is `0.85 × 1.20 − 1 = +2%`; if usage rises only 10%, revenue changes by −6.5%. These are scenario calculations, not observed industry demand elasticities. Costs must also be deducted from revenue before discussing profit.

Open source and new models can provide evidence of competitive pressure, but the compute bill for a particular training run is not a company's entire cost of research, data, experimentation, personnel and operations. Nor can one benchmark result establish that substitution has been completed across all commercial tasks. Until usage, reliability and customer switching costs are validated, a technical demonstration cannot be converted directly into a revenue-loss rate.

#### Asset Replacement Is Not the Same Kind of "Decline"

Declining well output drives the need for continued drilling. AI accelerators, even while working normally, may become less economical because of changes in cost per task, memory, interconnects or software compatibility. Older equipment can move to different workloads; genuine obsolescence must be assessed through secondary uses, rental prices and electricity costs, rather than assuming mandatory retirement at a certain accounting age.

Land, grid connections, buildings, cooling systems and chips within the same AI project cannot share one residual-value curve. Data-center and power assets may serve several generations of equipment, or may require retrofits because of regional oversupply or an inability to support new power densities. A cash flow model must allow both "efficiency gains extend economic use" and "new architectures accelerate replacement," rather than retaining only the path that supports a bubble judgment.

#### Policy and National Strategy: Changing Constraints Without Guaranteeing Returns

Shale oil is also affected by land, water, emissions, pipelines and transportation; AI is constrained by electricity, data, exports and product compliance. Both have policy variables that may change project costs, start dates and addressable sales. Regulatory tail risk cannot be described as unique to AI.

National-strategy emphasis may change project conditions through research support, permits, energy construction, procurement or financing instruments, but does not mean the government backstops every company's entire investment. Research must distinguish government policy, private equity funding, corporate commitments and sovereign capital transaction by transaction. In particular, an investment amount cannot be booked as fiscal expenditure already incurred simply because a project carries a national-strategy label.

This also means that a cycle need not end at a single market-clearing moment: some projects may continue because of strategic demand, while others remain constrained by returns and debt maturities. Support can change scenario weights, but belongs in a quantitative model only once amounts, duration and conditions have been obtained.

### V. Three Research Tasks Derived from the Historical Analogy

#### Verify Technological Success, Operating Success and Investment Success Separately

Technological success concerns task capability, adoption and productivity; operating success concerns paid retention, cash gross profit, return on capital and competitive position; investment success additionally considers entry valuation, financing costs, dilution and holding period. The three layers may materialize sequentially, or their benefits may accrue to different parties. Evidence for one layer cannot replace evidence for the other two.

Thus, "the technology is real" only refutes the claim that it is completely useless; it does not automatically refute overvaluation. "Some participants lose money" likewise does not prove failure of the entire technology's diffusion. To judge whether a view has been validated, state in advance which layer was predicted and what data will be used to score it.

#### Distinguish Three Paths of Stress Transmission

**The demand path**: paid retention or customer returns fall short of expectations, affecting new purchases and actual use; this may then transmit to operator revenue and orders for newly built equipment. Customer, contract or billable-usage data is needed; share prices alone cannot establish it.

**The price-and-efficiency path**: usage keeps rising, but prices for equivalent quality fall faster than costs improve, putting profit and equipment economic life under pressure. This path does not require demand to collapse and does not guarantee simultaneous damage to suppliers. Volume, price and unit cost must be examined at each layer.

**The financing-and-delivery path**: delays in electricity connection, construction, acceptance or lease commencement extend cash flow shortfalls; if refinancing tightens, some projects may be postponed or restructured. Long-term customer demand may still exist. Those first affected are participants with greater maturity mismatch and weaker financing buffers.

These three paths can reinforce one another, or be eased by retention growth, cost improvements, contractual protection or external funding. Separating them helps explain why strong orders, ambitious construction plans and credit pressure at individual companies can coexist in the same quarter.

#### Constrain the Analogy with Disconfirming Evidence

If end-customer cash benefits improve consistently, unsubsidized paid use expands, customers become less dependent on financing, and upstream suppliers continue receiving stable payments without new backstops, the judgment that the industry is "mainly financing-driven, with insufficient end-customer returns" should weaken.

If usage grows without corresponding improvement in the quality of paid demand, customer retention and cash profit, or if free cash flow improves mainly through sharp cuts in necessary reinvestment, one cannot lightly declare capital recovery complete. The shale-oil sample in which "investment contracted faster than cash flow" reminds us to separate the causes of cash improvement each time, rather than treating every move into positive cash flow as a recovery.

### VI. Who Can Retain the Gains: Four Roles Still Require Transaction-by-Transaction Testing

"Low-cost operators" are companies that can generate cash sustainably at lower selling prices, not a permanent title for any recognized leader. Customer retention, delivery reliability, cash from existing businesses and cost per task can support this role, but the entry valuation may still exhaust all the operating advantage in advance. The most dangerous combination of grand narratives and growth targets is one in which capacity, tokens or ARR keep rising while nobody explains how much additional cash is needed to sustain growth.

"Consolidators" need deployable cash and the ability to identify assets that can be integrated into their own businesses. Cheap GPUs lacking electricity or customers, or requiring expensive migration, are not necessarily cheap operable compute. Low-price acquisitions may also come with leases, guarantees, environmental obligations and demolition liabilities. Cash provides options, not guaranteed acquisition returns.

"Providers of scarce infrastructure" may include wafer fabrication, equipment, electricity supply, grid access and cooling, but the value of charging rights depends on the contract: usage-based charges, minimum payments, take-or-pay arrangements, government-approved returns and customer credit are not the same risk. Stopping compute workloads may save some electricity without ending PPA, data-center rent or debt-service obligations. If a customer defaults, contractual rights still require enforcement before becoming cash. Power or wafer capacity therefore cannot be called risk-free "land rights."

ASML's Q3 revenue and operating cash performance disclosed on October 14, 2026 was strong, providing evidence that the supply chain is realizing gains, contrary to the pessimistic case. But its cash flow includes working-capital changes; one quarter's cash cannot all be annualized, and an equipment supplier earning money in the current quarter does not establish that all customer projects will pay back. [ASML Q3 2026 US GAAP financial statements, income statement and cash flow statement](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/937966/000162828026065813/financial_statementsxusx.htm)

"Capital providers holding liquidity" can wait for conditions to improve, but waiting itself has an opportunity cost. Corporate operating cash, fund capital subject to investment mandates, government budgets and sovereign-fund commitments cannot all be treated as a pool of cash ready to enter AI projects at any time, still less used to name the ultimate winners.

### VII. Testing the Opposing Case Must Allow More Than a Later Bubble Collapse

This framework must allow five developments sufficient to change the original judgment: end-customer payments materialize faster than infrastructure; falling costs for equivalent-quality tasks expand demand and improve cash gross profit; supplier investments help customers through construction, after which customers no longer depend on injections; policy or long-term capital improves the maturity structure without continuing to expand low-return expenditure; and greater technological capability turns previously unbillable tasks into stable businesses. The first four do not require an announcement that AGI has arrived. The fifth also needs evidence from tasks, production deployment and paid retention; a single benchmark score cannot make the case.

These forms of disconfirming evidence affect revenue, cost, funding sources, maturities or asset uses respectively, and cannot substitute for one another. Rising share prices and successful financing mainly show that markets remain willing to supply capital; improving customer cash gross profit and renewals are closer to project economics. If disconfirming evidence persists, the corresponding capital-recovery risk assessment should be reduced, rather than explaining every case as "the longer it is propped up, the more dangerous the end."

Historical analogies offer no transferable uniform similarity score, amplification factor or number of years to clear the market. Whether AI projects ultimately recover their capital must be assessed through their own billable use, cost curves, delivery progress, financing terms and competitive landscape. The questions supplied by history still require answers in these companies' cash flows.

## Appendix D: Alphabet's Financing Case - From Planned Capacity to Per-Share Value

Information cutoff: 2026-10-31 (Beijing time). All amounts are in USD billions; share prices and earnings per share are in USD. The Q3 earnings release and September 30 10-Q have been published; the interim financial statements are unaudited. ATM issuance, bond financing and cash balances have been rolled forward from the Q2 baseline to Q3. Announced plans, completed issuance, actual cash and future optional capacity remain separately presented.

### D.1 The USD 80 Billion Plan Exists, but It Is Not USD 80 Billion Received at Once

This round of Alphabet financing has moved from planning into further execution: the initial 6/1 plan had an 80 ceiling; common and preferred stock financing generated net cash of 49.562 in June; Q3 brought a further 12.371 in net cash through the ATM program; and net cash from the two types of equity financing totaled 61.933 for the first nine months. Approximately 27.6 of ATM capacity remained as of 9/30. New shares, preferred-stock rights and debt interest have all entered existing shareholders' cash and per-share models. The current position can no longer be described as "the ATM has not yet been used."

The structure in the formal 6/1 filing comprised an underwritten offering of 30, an ATM program of 40 and a private placement of 10 to Berkshire affiliates; the underwritten portion included common stock and mandatory convertible preferred stock. This document establishes the financing plan at that time, not full execution, and still less that management determined operating cash would be insufficient for the next three years. [Initial 6/1 financing filing](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000119312526251733/d160205dfwp.htm)

| Document or event date | What happened | Treatment in reconciliation |
|---|---|---|
| Initial 6/1 plan | 30 underwritten offering, 40 ATM, 10 private placement | Planned structure; not booked directly as cash; final issuance size may vary |
| Common stock completed on 6/4 | Public offering of 29 million shares each of Class A and Class C; Berkshire private placement of 14 million shares of each class | Public-offering net proceeds approximately 20.5, private placement 10; total newly issued common shares of 86 million |
| Preferred stock completed on 6/5 | 6.25% mandatory convertible preferred stock | Notes disclose net proceeds of approximately 19.0; future conversion and dividends modeled separately |
| First-half cash flow statement | Net common-stock cash of 30.499, net preferred-stock cash of 19.063 | Total 49.562; financing cash, not operating revenue |
| ATM status as of 6/30 | No shares issued under the 40 authorization | Retained as a historical baseline, followed by Q3 execution below |
| August ATM supplement | Sales agents added; other terms unchanged | Neither an additional 40 nor proof of execution |
| Final 8/6 bond document | Listed 25 of senior unsecured notes at the time, with expected settlement on 8/10 | Prospective terms and expected date; subsequent actual proceeds use Q3 statements and are not simply added to the 6/30 balance |
| Q3 ATM completed | 12 million Class A shares and 24 million Class C shares, with respective net proceeds of approximately 4.3 and 8.1 | Exact net cash flow statement proceeds of 12.371; approximately 27.6 of capacity unused |
| Q3 bonds and total debt cash | Net senior unsecured note proceeds approximately 28.7; total net debt issuance proceeds of 28.996, debt repayment of 3.626 | Note proceeds include USD and AUD issuance; their scope differs from total debt cash, so they are not added again |

Sources: [Alphabet Q2 10-Q, cash flow statement, Note 11 and liquidity section](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000165204426000071/goog-20260630.htm), [ATM supplement](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000119312526336853/d140593d424b5.htm), [Final 8/6 bond prospectus](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000119312526340264/d32286d424b2.htm).

The difference between 49.562 in the cash flow statement and 49.5 from summing rounded note components reflects presentation precision, not an omitted piece of "mysterious financing." Common stock, preferred stock, employee shares and the statement of changes in shareholders' equity also have distinct accounting scopes. This analysis therefore uses the cash flow statement for cash and issuance notes for share counts, without mixing them into a custom precise figure.

This reconciliation now extends through September 30. Q3 ATM components of 4.3 and 8.1 are rounded disclosure figures; their sum cannot replace the cash flow statement's 12.371. Similarly, "net senior notes of 28.7" and "total net debt issuance of 28.996" have different scopes. USD note issuance included 23.8 at fixed rates and 1.3 at floating rates, alongside two AUD tranches, so the original August plan of 25 cannot be called the whole quarter's total financing. The execution above is reconciled between the Q3 10-Q and the accompanying release, but both remain disclosures from the same company and are not presented as two independent sources. [Alphabet Q3 10-Q, debt, equity and cash flows](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000165204426000084/goog-20260930.htm), [Q3 release, pages 2 and 6](https://s206.q4cdn.com/479360582/files/doc_financials/2026/q3/2026q3-alphabet-earnings-release.pdf)

### D.2 Ask "What Is the New Money For?" and Check "What Is the Existing Money?"

As of 9/30, Alphabet had cash and cash equivalents of 56.334 and marketable securities of 208.764, totaling 265.098. The securities included equity investments of 90.384, of which 75.2 was SpaceX stock subject to short-term sale restrictions. Cash, government and debt securities, and restricted equity are not the same form of liquidity. The figure 265.098 can describe the reported combined assets, but cannot all be called cash immediately available to pay construction bills. The carrying value of long-term debt of 125.102 at the same date also differs from note principal and includes the effects of discounts, hedges and current-portion classification. [Q3 10-Q, balance sheet, investment and debt notes](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000165204426000084/goog-20260930.htm)

The operating cash-timing pressure has continued beyond a single negative quarter: Q3 operating cash of 42.290 and cash capital expenditures of 51.355 produced FCF of −9.065, more negative than Q2's −5.855; trailing-twelve-month FCF remained +19.747. Cloud operating profit rose to 11.121 over the same period, showing that operating demand and group-level asset-payment pressure can rise simultaneously. Cloud profit cannot prove that every new batch of assets has paid back, nor can a negative group quarter be permanently extrapolated into an operating breakdown. [Official Q3 financial results and FCF reconciliation](https://s206.q4cdn.com/479360582/files/doc_financials/2026/q3/2026q3-alphabet-earnings-release.pdf)

Uses of proceeds should continue to be recorded separately according to the documents. Completed common- and preferred-stock proceeds are for general corporate purposes, including AI infrastructure; the ATM program is primarily for tax obligations related to employee equity awards. Q3 ATM execution does not change this distinction. Neither the 12.371 received nor the remaining 27.6 of capacity can therefore be allocated entirely to new AI data centers, and the entire group's cash shortfall cannot be attributed to AI.

Financing may simultaneously support construction, cash management, acquisitions and capital-structure choices. Only by aligning these uses with cash balances, debt maturities, operating expenditure and shareholder returns can one judge whether financing proactively adds headroom or fills a gap for payments that cannot be postponed. Issuing shares alone does not yield a unique answer.

### D.3 For Existing Shareholders, the Real Calculation Is Per-Share Value

Financing expands company assets but also adds claims for new shareholders or holders of preferred rights. Looking only at the amount raised or the increase in shares shows just one side. The test is whether the future cash purchased with those funds can exceed the additional claims and financing costs.

#### D.3.1 Common Stock: More Shares Do Not Automatically Mean Value Destruction

In a simplified model excluding preferred stock, let pre-financing common-equity value be V₀, the share count N₀, net issuance cash C, newly issued shares ΔN, and the net present value created by new projects after deducting investment NPV. Then:

> Post-financing value per share =（V₀ + C + NPV）/（N₀ + ΔN）.

If the proceeds remain on the balance sheet, first include them as cash; if they are invested in projects, project NPV must already deduct the investment, so that cash and the same project asset are not counted twice. Issuance expenses, taxes and control-right differences should also be included. Do not simply divide the financing amount by total market capitalization to claim a permanent percentage loss.

A purely illustrative example: initial value is 1000 and the share count 10, giving pre-financing value of 100 per share. Issuing 0.1 new shares at 100 each raises cash of 10. If project NPV is zero, post-financing value of 1010/10.1 remains 100 per share. Although each share owns a smaller proportion of the company, no value is lost under this no-cost assumption. If new-project NPV is −5, value per share is approximately 99.50; if it is +5, approximately 100.50. This is not an Alphabet price target; it only illustrates why financing price and project returns must be calculated together.

The June public common-stock offering and private placement added 86 million shares, and the Q3 ATM issued another 36 million. Their combined 122 million shares can be used as the issuance volume for these specific financing transactions. It cannot be added again to the net increase in common shares for the entire period, which also includes employee shares, repurchases and other items. Period-end common shares at 9/30 were 12.294 billion, while Q3 diluted weighted-average shares were 12.368 billion. They measure different populations and time weights; the quarter's EPS cannot be reverse-engineered directly from the increase in period-end shares. [Q3 10-Q, equity and EPS notes](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000165204426000084/goog-20260930.htm)

#### D.3.2 Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock: Check Dividends and Conversion Terms First

The preferred stock carries a 6.25% dividend, payable in cash, common stock or a combination at the company's option. Mandatory conversion normally occurs in May 2029, with the share count depending on the price range of the relevant share class and anti-dilution provisions. The company also paid approximately 1.0 for a capped call to reduce some conversion dilution. This is not cost-free funding, nor can it be broadly described as "no current dilution and no additional burden ever." [Q2 10-Q, Note 11 and earnings-per-share note](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000165204426000071/goog-20260630.htm)

Q3 recognized preferred-stock dividends of 0.298; subtracting these from net income of 26.468 left income attributable to common stock of 26.170. This shows that preferred rights already affect the current common-share earnings measure; one cannot focus only on the share count at future conversion. This is also a separate item from non-operating gains and losses caused by fair-value changes in the company's equity securities, and each should enter the per-share bridge separately. [Q3 10-Q, earnings-per-share note](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000165204426000084/goog-20260930.htm)

The research model should separately list current preferred dividends, conversion share counts, the capped call's effective range and cost, and follow the EPS note's anti-dilution treatment. Without a tranche-by-tranche model, no reliable range for permanent dilution or future EPS changes can be provided.

| Financing instrument | Rights and cash to list first | Position in the existing-shareholder model |
|---|---|---|
| Common stock | Net cash proceeds, new shares, issue price and expenses | Cash or project value enters the numerator; additional shares enter the denominator |
| Mandatory convertible preferred stock | Cumulative dividends, conversion date, price range, anti-dilution and capped call | Periodic dividends and future conversion shares, with EPS rules checked separately |
| ATM capacity | Actual issuance dates, shares, net proceeds and remaining capacity | No cash received recorded before execution; no assumption of all dilution at once |
| Fixed-rate bonds | Net proceeds, coupons, maturities and covenants | Interest, debt-repayment cash and financing repricing; no direct addition to common shares |
| Floating-rate loans | Reference rate, spread, repricing date and balance | Rate changes flow through on contractual dates; policy rates are not applied directly |

Bond financing adds interest and principal due at maturity, but does not directly increase common shares. Common stock, preferred stock and bonds cannot be placed in a universally valid ranking "from cheapest to most expensive": financing timing, issue price, tax shields, rating constraints and investment returns all change the actual cost.

### D.4 Evidence Boundaries for External Investor Participation

This review reread Note 11 of the Q2 10-Q and confirmed Berkshire affiliates' participation in the private placement of 10. It is an external funding source. Investing in Alphabet does not turn it into an internal loop between cloud providers and model companies, still less an "endorsement at an above-fair-value price" without a pricing comparison.

This investment establishes only that an institution accepted the relevant price and rights. It does not prove optimism about all AI assets, nor replace a calculation of Alphabet's project returns. Who participated in the decision, why this company was selected, and whether other companies were reduced at the same time each require their own public statements or holdings evidence. Inferring from a single private placement that a particular individual has concluded other giants will collapse is not a verifiable conclusion.

Further research on Berkshire should obtain quarterly 13Fs, check share classes, share counts and reporting dates, and separate purchases from changes in the market value of holdings. Quarter-end disclosures still do not equal real-time positions on October 31, and actual investment cannot be inferred from differences in market value. This appendix does not incorporate a holdings trajectory whose verification remains incomplete.

### D.5 Historical Comparisons Should Compare Rights, Not Guess the Top

Historical financing comparisons must first establish whether the instruments are alike: common stock's residual claim, preferred dividends and conversion terms, upside participation through attached warrants, and debt's repayment at maturity carry different risks. Valuations at financing, counterparty liquidity and investors' bargaining positions also need to be compared together. Preferred rights do not equal risk-free protection, and participation through common stock does not mean an absence of investment value.

Studying the timing ability of financing decisions requires checking original issuance documents transaction by transaction, including issuance cases that were not followed by a crash, and harmonizing event windows, prices or total returns, market benchmarks and sample selection. Searching backward for financing only among companies that subsequently collapsed creates selection bias; it does not establish that Alphabet will follow the same path after financing.

The lessons worth retaining are these: financing addresses the ability to pay but does not automatically solve investment returns; participation by a powerful investor does not eliminate subsequent capital losses; and equity financing may improve debt-paying capacity, making construction more resilient to volatility. Whether it benefits or dilutes existing shareholders must be decided jointly by the issue price, use of proceeds and cash recovery.

The October 28 FOMC decision held the range at 3.75%-4.00%, with two dissenters favoring a 25-basis-point increase; this supersedes the previous edition's "next decision pending" status. Outstanding fixed coupons, floating-rate repricing, future financing and the equity discount rate work through different mechanisms. The policy range cannot be added directly to every Alphabet debt instrument, nor does an unchanged rate mean financing costs have stopped changing. [Official FOMC October 28 decision](https://www.federalreserve.gov/newsevents/pressreleases/monetary20261028a.htm)

### D.6 How the Next Financial Report Would Change This Appendix's Conclusion

| Subsequent evidence | Where to update | Inference to avoid |
|---|---|---|
| Subsequent issuance, shares and expenses against the remaining 27.6 of ATM capacity | Roll forward from the 12.371 already executed in Q3 | Do not treat additional sales agents as additional funds |
| New debt, repayments, floating-rate repricing and debt balances | Continue rolling borrowing cash and maturity schedules from Q3 disclosed receipts | Do not treat a final term sheet as a cash receipt |
| AI assets entering service, Cloud profits and working capital | New-project cash-recovery model | Do not attribute all Cloud profit to assets newly built in that quarter |
| Preferred dividends, conversion and capped-call changes | Share-count and earnings-per-share bridge | Do not book all maximum dilution into the current period at once |
| Cash from commissioned assets consistently exceeding budget | Raise project net present value or shorten payback | Do not maintain a permanently bearish conclusion on the basis of a single financing transaction |
| Revenue delays while mandatory payments and financing costs rise | Lower project net present value and reassess liquidity | Do not directly declare that the company has already defaulted |

This appendix concludes that financing has progressed further into execution: ATM issuance increases common shares, preferred stock generates current dividends, and multicurrency bonds broaden debt funding sources. At the same time, Cloud profit is growing while the group's construction cash shortfall is widening. The initial 80 cannot all be treated as received, but financing can no longer be described merely as a plan. Whether existing shareholders benefit must be decided jointly by issue price, use of proceeds, project net cash and per-share rights. This appendix does not infer a crash date or individual-stock drawdown range from financing size.

The Treasury's October 30 ten-year nominal yield was 4.94% and its ten-year real yield 2.60%, both down 6 basis points from the previous edition's October 26 readings of 5.00% and 2.66%. This is a change in the market discount benchmark, not Alphabet's locked-in blended financing cost, and does not change fixed-debt coupons. October 31 was a Saturday; no closing observation for that day is fabricated here. Quarterly financials still run through September 30; market interest rates cannot automatically extend the reporting period by a month. [Treasury nominal yields, October 30 row](https://home.treasury.gov/resource-center/data-chart-center/interest-rates/TextView?field_tdr_date_value_month=202610&type=daily_treasury_yield_curve), [Real yields, October 30 row](https://home.treasury.gov/resource-center/data-chart-center/interest-rates/TextView?field_tdr_date_value_month=202610&type=daily_treasury_real_yield_curve)

## Appendix E: Fact Checks and Evidence Boundaries

This table supports the current V1.9 judgments, with information through 31 October 2026, Beijing time; interest rates use US observations for 30 October. F01-F16 carry forward the verified V1.8 baseline; this review deepens video-model, microdrama-duration, and coding-labor-cost research. A higher version number does not mean every historical source was reacquired or quarterly financial statements externally audited. Unless otherwise stated, B means billion and M means million.

The body distinguishes disclosed facts, company forward-looking statements, third-party estimates, the author's conditional illustrations, and unverified matters. Company IR materials, SEC statements, and republications can cross-check excerpts, but one disclosing entity does not create several independent sets of operating evidence. Cross-review tests reasoning; it does not replace auditors or evidence of end-customer cash.

### E.1 Key Facts Currently Used and Areas Deepened in This Review

**F01 | Microsoft: Quarters, Segments, and Leases.** FY27Q1 results announced on 28 October reported revenue of USD 92.279B and operating profit of 45.915B for the period ended 30 September. Operating cash of 58.000B less cash capital purchases of 39.367B gives 18.633B; deducting another 1.000B of finance-lease principal gives 17.633B. The latter is this report's explicitly bounded cash bridge, not a newly defined uniform company FCFF. Earnings-call CapEx, new finance leases, and 10-Q additions to right-of-use assets differ in scope and cannot be forced to reconcile or deducted twice. Company-restated figures are used for the new fiscal year's segment reorganization and Azure definition changes; old “Azure and other cloud services” figures cannot simply be spliced in. [Original results](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/Investor/earnings/FY-2027-Q1/press-release-webcast). Lease notes and restatement details are in the earnings chapter; differences remain where complete bridges were unavailable.

**F02 | Alphabet: Separate Operating Growth from Investment Losses.** 2026 Q3 results announced on 28 October reported Cloud revenue of 30.709B and operating profit of 11.121B; group operating profit was 40.576B. Pretax equity-security losses of 8.664B cannot be subtracted directly from after-tax net income. Q3 operating cash of 42.290B less cash capital purchases of 51.355B gives −9.065B; TTM is 179.551−159.804＝+19.747B. A negative quarter and a still-positive annual period must both be stated. Current financials replace Q2 as the current baseline, while Q2 remains for like-for-like comparisons. [Company-hosted results PDF](https://s206.q4cdn.com/479360582/files/doc_financials/2026/q3/2026q3-alphabet-earnings-release.pdf), [Q3 10-Q](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1652044/000165204426000084/goog-20260930.htm). Both belong to the same company disclosure system.

**F03 | Amazon: Cloud Growth Has Not Eliminated the Construction Funding Gap.** 2026 Q3 results announced on 29 October reported AWS revenue of 45.984B and operating profit of 17.773B; the Q3 cash bridge is 42.422−(77.259−1.381)＝−33.456B. TTM operating cash of 168.300B less net cash capital purchases of 210.657B gives −42.357B, rounded to −42.4B in the release. The old TTM figure of −7.604B ends on 30 June and is not added to the current-quarter gap. Company-defined FCF has not deducted all lease principal and financing uses and is not the full debt-service funding gap. [Company Q3 results and reconciliation](https://ir.aboutamazon.com/news-release/news-release-details/2026/Amazon-com-Announces-Third-Quarter-Results/default.aspx), [AP independent editorial report](https://apnews.com/article/2de72b65d63006a7ce85eab441e2d6b7). AP corrected the negative 42.4B figure, consistent in direction and rounding; media repetition is not independent proof of customer collections.

**F04 | Chinese Optical Modules: Official Quarterly Reports Replace Half-Year Observations.** Innolight and Eoptolink published quarterly reports on 30 October, and TFC's original appeared on 29 October; all cover unaudited cumulative results for the first three quarters of 2026. The simple operating-cash-minus-long-term-asset-purchase balances are −RMB 1.15243 billion, −RMB 341.59 million, and +RMB 970.83 million respectively. Innolight's Q3-only cash bridge improved; Eoptolink's Q3-only operating cash was negative. Cumulative, single-quarter, and attributable-profit figures are not mixed. These balances exclude other investing, dividends, and debt repayment and cannot be called complete financing gaps. [Innolight](https://static.cninfo.com.cn/finalpage/2026-10-30/1225643953.PDF), [Eoptolink](https://static.cninfo.com.cn/finalpage/2026-10-30/1225642947.PDF), [TFC](https://static.cninfo.com.cn/finalpage/2026-10-29/1225634414.PDF). Half-year figures and yuan-by-yuan recalculations are in the China transmission chapter.

**F05 | Longsys: From Preliminary Guidance to an Archived Quarterly Report.** This review obtained an archived copy of the official quarterly report: first-three-quarter revenue of RMB 38.718 billion, net profit attributable to parent-company shareholders of RMB 14.322 billion, and operating cash of −RMB 2.274 billion; the company states Q3 operating cash was +RMB 877 million. Direct links at CNINFO or the exchange and cash capital-purchase details have not yet been fully checked, so the source remains classified as an archived announcement; complete FCF verification is not claimed. [Archived original](https://money.finance.sina.com.cn/corp/view/vCB_AllBulletinDetail.php?id=12680548&stockid=301308), [National Business Daily report](https://www.nbd.com.cn/articles/2026-10-29/4628749.html). Positive quarterly and still-negative cumulative cash are both retained; neither cancels the other.

**F06 | Vertiv: Updated Cash and Guidance Bridge.** Q3 disclosures on 28 October reported revenue of USD 4312.8M, operating cash of 794.0M, and adjusted FCF of 652.2M. First-three-quarter adjusted FCF of 2230.3M versus full-year guidance of 2600-2800M implies Q4 of 369.7-569.7M. The full-year midpoint and EPS are company forward-looking figures; 25/30/35 times are only author conditional sensitivities, not target prices. Detailed announcements, non-GAAP reconciliations, and secondary checks are in the valuation chapter; detailed cumulative items remain company single-source disclosures.

**F07 | Financing and Model Commercialization: Status Matters More Than Nominal Amount.** Anthropic's public S-1 does not mean an effective offering, closing, or cash proceeds received. Microsoft and Alphabet's restated operating metrics and equity-financing execution replace old anchors according to current disclosures. API credits, research support, and public-sector discounts support adoption but are not equal amounts of incremental net billing. New-model or partnership announcements do not prove long-task reliability, ROI for all customers, or sustained gross margin. Original materials, publication times, and unverified items are in the circular-investment, monetization, AGI, and Alphabet chapters; no uniform “total circular financing” is set.

**F08 | FOMC: A Future Event Becomes a Published Outcome.** At 14:00 US daylight time on 28 October (02:00 Beijing time on 29 October), the committee voted 10-2 to hold 3.75%-4.00%; the two dissenters favored a 25-basis-point increase. This was not a rate cut and cannot be described as an additional increase already implemented. [Federal Reserve official statement](https://www.federalreserve.gov/newsevents/pressreleases/monetary20261028a.htm).

**F09 | Macro: Separate Measurement Periods from Release Dates.** September PCE prices rose +0.5% month on month and +3.6% year on year, with core +0.3%/+3.1%, released on 29 October; this is not “October PCE.” GDP, ECI, and initial/revised-estimate boundaries are detailed in Chapter 11. Macro aggregates do not identify AI's separate contribution to growth or inflation. [BEA September personal income and outlays](https://www.bea.gov/news/2026/personal-income-and-outlays-september-2026).

**F10 | Treasuries: 30 October Observations, Not Corporate Borrowing Rates.** The 2-year nominal, 10-year nominal, and 10-year real yields were 4.56%, 4.94%, and 2.60%, versus 4.68%, 5.00%, and 2.66% on 26 October, changes of −12, −6, and −6 basis points. Corporate credit spreads, fixed coupons, collateral, and WACC cannot be shifted one for one. [Treasury nominal curve](https://home.treasury.gov/resource-center/data-chart-center/interest-rates/TextView?field_tdr_date_value_month=202610&type=daily_treasury_yield_curve), [real curve](https://home.treasury.gov/resource-center/data-chart-center/interest-rates/TextView?field_tdr_date_value_month=202610&type=daily_treasury_real_yield_curve).

**F11 | AI Video Microdramas: Keep the Whole Industry and AI Subset Strictly Separate.** Sensor Tower's approximately USD 750M global microdrama-app IAP and more than 850M downloads in 2026 Q1 are app-store measurement estimates, not the US market, pure-AI revenue, advertising revenue, or distributor net profit. Omdia's approximately USD 1.5B for US microdramas in 2026 is an all-category annual forecast. Conversion and retention for HOLYWATER's two apps belong to the operator's sample and cannot represent all Americans or all AI microdramas. Chapter 4 consolidates these boundaries, company exposures, copyright, and unit economics. [Sensor Tower research](https://sensortower.com/blog/state-of-short-drama-apps-2026-report), [Omdia forecast](https://omdia.tech.informa.com/pr/2026/feb/microdramas-overtake-streamers-on-mobile-engagement-says-omdia), [HOLYWATER report entry page](https://www.holywater.tech/report). Different coverage is not used to calculate supposed AI microdrama penetration.

**F11 Supplement | Company Revenue and Sustained Returns.** Duiba separately reported RMB 222.629M of AI microdrama revenue in 2026 H1, while the group lost 25.326M. Its 14 October announcement only preliminarily assessed unaudited management accounts, expecting monthly net profit for the business in July-September without amounts. COL's H1 microdrama/IP-derivative revenue of RMB 411.260M is not AI-specific. 9M operating cash of 42.796M minus approximately 126.802M in H1 gives approximately −RMB 84.006M in Q3 alone; group fluctuations cannot be attributed solely to AI. The 49.24% indirect CMS interest is an equity-method relationship, not the proportion of ReelShort revenue consolidated; CMS data used for current investment income cover only January-March. Chapter 4 retains original page references and periods. [Duiba interim report](https://yun.duiba.com.cn/corp/report/content/1/2026%E4%B8%AD%E6%9C%9F%E5%A0%B1%E5%91%8A.3f87c290d81d43e1b87da58f7eaf9818.pdf), [Duiba operating announcement](https://www.hkexnews.hk/listedco/listconews/sehk/2026/1014/2026101400004_c.pdf), [COL half-year report](https://static.cninfo.com.cn/finalpage/2026-08-26/1225504423.PDF), [third-quarter report](https://static.cninfo.com.cn/finalpage/2026-10-26/1225620142.PDF).

**F12 | Future Calendar: Remove Completed Events.** Cloud-company results, calls, and the FOMC on 28-30 October are no longer future catalysts. The November US release calendar uses standard time, EST, 13 hours behind Beijing. Whether complete statements were obtained is recorded separately; a call having occurred does not establish that all its data have been verified. [BLS November schedule](https://www.bls.gov/schedule/2026/11_sched.htm).

**F13 | Additional Supply-Chain Checks: Quarterly Cash Summaries Replace Waiting for Guidance.** Samsung's Q3 presentation shows 98.6−26.9＝KRW 71.7 trillion; SK hynix's Q3 presentation shows 54.781−12.670＝KRW 42.111 trillion, or 41.975 after another 0.136 of lease-liability repayments. These are simple cash bridges, without deducting all other investing and financing uses; presentations are not complete audited annual reports. Intel's simple cash bridge for the quarter ended 26 September is 5.082−4.219−0.119＝USD 0.744B. Its adjusted FCF of 1.639B additionally includes government capital incentives of 0.048B and net partner contributions of 0.847B. Financing support and customer operating receipts cannot be conflated. Originals, page references, and gaps in complete notes for the three companies are in the earnings chapter. The added AMD call is scheduled for 17:00 US Eastern on 3 November, 06:00 Beijing on the 4th; results are not filled in early.

**F14 | Old Coding Forecasts: Preserve the Original Proposition before Judging It.** Chapter 5 restores the original volume and main draft from the V1.0 Git commit snapshot, specifying the commit date, PDF footer date, and physical pages. 36.5 million×USD 30×12＝13.14B, not $131B; the original volume's high-price scenarios conflict with its USD 17.5 billion “absolute ceiling.” V1.5 already changed this to mechanical sensitivity, so this review does not claim the first withdrawal. Usage billing, nonprofessional uses, and package changes disconfirm the old boundary, but product ARR, activity, and targets cannot be assembled into realized industry revenue. 3.94B and 11.66B are uncalibrated conditional 2027 calculations, not a new forecast range. Original locations, formulas, and market evidence are in 5.1-5.6.

**F15 | Other Applications: Entities and Stages of Realization Cannot Be Omitted.** AlphaSense's Q2 figure above 0.7B is whole-platform ARR; ElevenAgents' threefold growth is not ElevenLabs' overall ARR. Duolingo's Q2 FCF of 88.260−2.734−6.896＝USD 78.630M belongs to the complete education product. Tempus net income includes unrealized securities gains; Alibaba AI Labs and Applications includes both model laboratories and applications. Neither can be simplified into a pure-AI-application profitability conclusion. Character.ai court testimony is debtor disclosure, stronger than anonymous rumors but not an independently audited monthly report; a Chapter 11 sale process is neither service termination nor a completed sale. Originals and pages are in Chapter 6.

**F16 | Technology, Licenses, and Prices: Availability Scope Is Also a Fact.** Gemini 4 Argon has opened API access; the launch-promotion footnotes specify that the promotion ends on 30 November; Carbon's initial restricted access is not general commercial availability. DeepSeek's old Flash alias now routes to V4.1-Flash-1017, with associated server-side Search/Fetch calls scheduled to begin charging at 00:00 Beijing time on 1 November; these are not counted as new October charges. Qwen open weights carry specific commercial-license conditions and cannot uniformly be called MIT. Independent experiments retain their older-model, small-sample, and no-feedback qualifications and are not extrapolated directly to all tasks at end-October. Sources and original licenses are in Chapters 3, 7, and 8 and the technical-update record.

**F17 | Microdrama Duration: Four Denominators Are Not Interchangeable.** QuestMobile's Hongguo 125.0 minutes is February 2026 “daily usage time per monthly user,” not one continuous session or daily usage by the entire population. The association's 120.5 minutes is the August 2025 chart value, not a January-August average; “may already have exceeded” long video is the original report's inference. Omdia's 35.7 versus 24.8 minutes concerns respective US mobile-app users of ReelShort and Netflix in 2025 Q4, not television, computers, cinemas, or total viewing. 80 episodes×1.5 minutes＝120 minutes is only a title-length example. Sources and dissemination are in 4.2 and the separate duration verification; usage time is not multiplied by monthly active users and calendar days to fabricate totals. [QuestMobile](https://www.questmobile.com.cn/research/report/2041710682848727041/), [association original](https://www.cnsa.cn/module/download/downfile.jsp?classid=0&filename=d2617fc1d3d742248c26765b0230b970.pdf), [Omdia](https://omdia.tech.informa.com/pr/2026/feb/microdramas-overtake-streamers-on-mobile-engagement-says-omdia)

**F18 | Video Versions and Prices: Record by Entry Point and SKU.** This review observes products including Seedance 2.5, MiniMax H3, and Kling 4.0 readable on 31 October; a 30- or 15-second single generation is not one-click generation of a whole film. Official H3 768p output costs USD 0.08/second; inputs, reference images, and regeneration have separate rules, and legacy Hailuo packages do not yet apply to H3. Kling's direct 4.0 price list was not obtained; Cloudflare's USD 0.157/second is an independent-channel price. Units in Google's enterprise table were not fully confirmed, so old Preview prices are not substituted as current prices. Product release, consumer availability, and enterprise API availability must be checked separately. [Volcano price list](https://docs.volcengine.com/docs/ark/model-pricing?lang=zh), [MiniMax price list](https://platform.minimax.io/docs/pricing/overview), [Cloudflare channel](https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai/models/klingai/kling-v4/)

**F19 | MiniMax Financials: Separate Video, Platform, and Group.** Hailuo product revenue of USD 17.464M, or 32.6%, in the first nine months of 2025 is not all video revenue or microdrama revenue for the period. In 2026 H1, group revenue was 116.573M, platform/enterprise services 73.929M, gross profit 20.813M, R&D 296.870M, and non-IFRS adjusted net loss 293.031M. H3 launched in late July and cannot explain H1. The platform's 63.4% includes multiple modalities; July total-token growth is not equal growth in video revenue or physical compute. Broad cash of USD 1.3228 billion differs from USD 930.9 million of cash equivalents; losses are not direct substitutes for cash burn. Original pages are in 4.8. [Prospectus](https://www1.hkexnews.hk/listedco/listconews/sehk/2025/1231/2025123100025.pdf), [interim results](https://www1.hkexnews.hk/listedco/listconews/sehk/2026/0826/2026082600680.pdf)

**F20 | Upstream Video Price-Volume Model: Scenario Scale Is Not a Market Forecast.** The 120 minutes, 80% AI share, 25% reuse, 720 advertising seconds, 960 localization seconds, 25% acceptance yield, and 90% billed share in 4.12 are all assumptions; the public H3 output price supplies only a price reference. Newly accepted volume is 6000 seconds, successful output 24000 seconds, billed output 21600 seconds, and narrowly defined output revenue USD 1728. Retries are already in the acceptance-yield denominator and cannot be multiplied again; free volume still incurs inference costs. With prices halved and acceptance yield doubled in 4.19, volume k corresponds to revenue k/4 and assumed contribution 0.1875k, requiring k of 4 and approximately 5.33 respectively to break even. Compute efficiency, cost changes, and incremental demand are not described as measured facts. Internal group calls are not consolidated external revenue; repeat viewing does not mean repeated generation.

**F21 | Coding Organizational Evidence: Do Not Attach New Coefficients to Old Samples.** Chen-Stratton uses the 28 October revision with 655 companies, 720542 workers cumulatively observed, and approximately 445 million events, not the old 718-company sample. Agent coefficients are incremental to earlier assistants; lines of code, issues, epics, and employment have distinct definitions and statistical intervals. No significant detected employment effect does not mean zero. NBER's cumulative commit effects for completion, interactive agents, and autonomous agents are not summed to 360%. The 4867-person RCT's +26.08% task-output effect implies approximately 20.685% time savings for equal output only under very strong comparability assumptions, not 26.08% wage savings. METR's old-model negative findings and subsequent selection bias are both retained. [Chen-Stratton](https://fion.ac/jellyfish.pdf), [NBER original](https://www.ftc.gov/system/files/ftc_gov/pdf/musolffdemireryang.pdf), [RCT](https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/10.1287/mnsc.2025.00535)

**F22 | Labor and Wages: Do Not Rename Descriptive Gaps as Causal Layoff Rates.** Stanford's 19% relative employment-path gap for young workers in highly exposed occupations is concentrated in hiring, includes multiple occupations, and is not a causal estimate. The Fed employment-trend gap, Epoch task-transfer survey, and announced company cuts retain their own definitions and do not yield a Chinese displacement rate. US BLS occupational medians, Chinese National Bureau of Statistics industry averages, and professional/technical-personnel means cannot be directly compared or added into a global wage pool; employer benefits cannot use one universal percentage either. Original periods, samples, and definitions remain in 5.9-5.10. [Stanford](https://digitaleconomy.stanford.edu/app/uploads/2026/08/Canaries_August2026.pdf), [BLS](https://www.bls.gov/ooh/Computer-and-Information-Technology/software-developers.htm), [National Bureau of Statistics](https://www.stats.gov.cn/sj/zxfb/202605/t20260515_1963707.html)

**F23 | Labor ROI: Keep Capacity, Cash, and Risk Budgets Separate.** The US 10-person and Chinese 8-person teams are author assumptions, including adoption, gross time savings, and incremental review, rework, and governance. Steady-state net hours released are 2400/900, with net capacity values of USD 200000/RMB 30000. Only explicit conditions for avoidable hiring, outsourcing, or similar expenditure produce the alternative cash paths of USD 140000/RMB 108000; these cannot be added. First-year learning and integration are deducted separately, and risk allowances are not described as actual incident payments. Fixed-budget and fixed-headcount formulas illustrate capacity without bottlenecks and with continuously adjustable resources. Demand elasticity is uncalibrated; no industry job-decline rate follows. Complete inputs, sensitivities, and formula-by-formula recalculations are in 5.11-5.14 and the calculation script.

### E.2 Retain Dated Baselines without Repackaging Them as New Facts

| Subject | Content retained in this edition | Verification boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Meta 2026 Q3 | Operating cash 32.786B less cash capital purchases 33.942B and lease principal 0.964B gives FCF −2.120B; first three quarters +11.049B | Disclosed on 21 October and already included in V1.7; not called new results in this review |
| TSMC 2026 Q3 | USD revenue 46.72B, gross margin 66.4%, simple TWD cash bridge 476.570B | Preserve currency and quarter separately; HPC does not equal pure AI |
| ASML 2026 Q3 | Operating profit, equity-security gains, and cash adjustments | Balance-sheet movements are not all customer advances or standard working capital |
| NVIDIA FY27Q2 | Demand, receivables turnover, concentration, and operating cash 24.077B | November forecasts are not filled in as results; anonymous customers are not guessed |
| Anthropic 2026 H1 S-1 | Unaudited revenue 16.286B, operating result −0.693B, operating cash +3.929B | Net loss includes noncash measurement; these half-year figures are not incremental October revenue |
| Oracle FY27Q1, Broadcom FY26Q3, CoreWeave financing | Procurement, leases, guarantees, advance payments, and drawdown status | Multiyear nominal contract values are not added into currently due debt |
| Demingli, first three quarters of 2026 | Revenue, inventories, and cumulative cash absorption | Already included on 26 October; a simple funds bridge is not a complete financing gap |
| Historical frameworks and cross-border tools | Original cases and conditional calculations | The cross-border legal baseline remains 27 October; current contracts across all jurisdictions were not individually rereviewed |
| Fusion energy | Helion Orion and CFS SPARC targets reread on 31 October | Initial operations in 2028 and Q>1 in 2027 are not realized commercial electricity sales |

Complete original links for each baseline remain in the relevant text and unified reference index. This table does not claim that the original 112 historical audit items or all old citations were reverified. Original versions remain separately available for traceability.

### E.3 Claims Excluded from Current Conclusions

- Industry-wide AI returns without independent evidence of end-customer payments, or undeduplicated totals of circular financing, procurement, and guarantees.
- Inferring realized revenue from downloads, plays, popularity, or surveyed willingness to pay; inferring an AI-generation market from all microdramas; calling global or ex-China data US data.
- Directly translating lower production cost into higher margins while omitting acquisition, refunds, failed productions, labor, and rights costs; replacing industry averages with platform-reported hits.
- Presenting company guidance, third-party forecasts, annualized run rates, or scenario illustrations as realized financial results; concealing single-source access restrictions as “multiple-party verification.”
- Declaring insolvency or collapsing demand directly from negative FCF; proving capital returns from completed financing, an IPO filing, or a large contract.
- Generating new target prices, default probabilities, and crash dates from old prices, currently unverified CDS, or arbitrary scenario weights.
- Retrofitting old conditional seat illustrations into forecasts with specific target years, or rewriting the original draft's longstanding acknowledgment of charges across fields as “we once forecast that only coding could earn money.”
- Generalizing subproduct ARR, group revenue, user activity, or court-restructuring status to an entire industry; conflating API promotions, restricted previews, open weights, and unconditional commercial licenses.
- Renaming mobile-app usage per user as total film viewing across all devices; calculating video-model generation demand from repeat viewing.
- Treating work output, jobs, wages, resource value, and avoidable cash as one metric; upgrading observational employment gaps into AI-caused layoff proportions.

| Innolight (8008444194.49−9160875564.49)÷100 million | −11.5243 × RMB 100 million | Cumulative simple cash bridge |
| Eoptolink (1464429142.65−1806016532.89)÷100 million | −3.4159 × RMB 100 million | Cumulative simple cash bridge |
| TFC (1461851321.62−491017854.77)÷100 million | +9.7083 × RMB 100 million | Cumulative simple cash bridge |
